## Personalized warm-up ramps learned from logged history (owner idea — 2026-06-21)

**NEW — owner direction.** Atlas should *always* suggest a ramp on main compounds (confirmed good), but when a user has logged history it should recognize **their** ramp pattern instead of always prescribing the generic `buildWarmupRamp` (50/70/85% @ 8/5/3, fixed 3 steps).

**Live evidence (owner history, Thu Jun 18):** Bench Press logged as `135×15 @6 → 185×10 @2 → 225×8 @1 → 225×5 @2 → 225×5 @2` — the user ramps with ~2 warm-up sets at **higher reps** than the generic ramp and **tags warm-ups with high RIR** (`@6`); accessories (Seated Row / Weighted Dip / Face Pull, all `@2` flat) get no ramp. So warm-up sets are **cleanly detectable in `Log_Cleaned`** via high RIR (reps-in-reserve ≥ ~4–5) + ascending load into a top set.

**Design (deterministic, engine-first, no LLM):**
- A pure analytics pass (`services/`) that, per lift, infers the user's ramp **shape** from history — number of warm-up sets, load steps as %-of-top-set, and rep scheme — and prescribes a ramp matching it, scaled to the current working weight and rounded to plate increments.
- **Fallback to the generic `buildWarmupRamp`** when there's no confident personal pattern (too few sessions, or no logged warm-ups) — never fabricate a personalized ramp from thin data. The generic ramp stays the floor so users without history still get one.
- Selection layered into `attachMainCompoundWarmups` (it already owns which lifts ramp): personalized shape if confident, else generic.

**Open design questions:** (a) confidence threshold — min sessions with a detectable ramp before overriding generic; (b) per-lift vs per-movement-pattern learning; (c) recency/EWMA weighting if the pattern drifts.

**Trust-critical adjacent check — DONE (2026-06-21).** Pre-check verdict: the **recommended weight is safe** — `recommendNextSet` anchors `nextWeight` on the *last* logged set (a working set, since warm-ups are logged first), `best_weight`/`first_weight` are `max`-based (warm-up-aware), and `detectStalls` already uses per-session max e1RM. **One real skew found + fixed:** `recommendNextSet`'s e1RM *trend* (`sessionBests`) used each session's **first** set (a warm-up for warm-up-first loggers), masking working-set progression — now uses the per-session max e1RM (mirrors `detectStalls`). Shipped as the prerequisite trend fix (regression tests in `test/analytics-edge.test.js`). Remaining minor note: `last_working_sets` (the displayed "recent sets") can still include warm-up rows for short sessions — display-only, no number-skew; a clean warm-up detector (PR 2 below) can label them later. `[trust-critical — resolved]`

**Relates to:** the existing "Preference learning from edits" item (this is preference-learning from *logged sets* rather than from plan edits). **PR sequence:** ✅ (0) trust pre-check + e1RM-trend fix (shipped, PR #460); ✅ (1) **warm-up-set detector** — `services/warmupDetector.js::detectWarmupRampShape(logRows, { liftCode | exerciseName })` returns a learned `{ steps:[{load_fraction, reps}], confidence, ramped_sessions, total_sessions }` or null. Pure/unwired. Per-session top = max-e1RM working set; warm-ups = lighter ascending lead-in sets before it (load-primary; RIR only excludes a near-failure lighter set); learns the most-recent ramp shape; null below `minRampedSessions` (2) so it never fabricates a pattern. Golden fixtures seeded from the owner's Jun 18 Bench data (`135×15@6 → 185×10@2 → 225` → 2-step ~0.60/0.82 ramp) + flat-accessory/back-off/no-RIR/near-failure cases (`test/warmupDetector.test.js`). Also fixed a real parse bug found here: a blank RIR cell now reads as **absent (null)**, not `0`. ✅ (2) **personalized-ramp builder** — `buildPersonalizedRamp(workingWeight, shape)` (`services/sessionBuilder.js`) scales the learned load fractions + rep scheme to today's working weight, rounds to plate increments, keeps the ramp strictly ascending and sub-working, returns `[]` (→ generic fallback) for a missing/invalid shape. ✅ (3) **wired**: `attachMainCompoundWarmups` / `structureSession` take an optional `rampShapeFor(exercise)`; `scoreIntents` passes a per-lift-code-cached `detectWarmupRampShape(logRows, …)` lookup into all four training intents, so each main compound uses the lifter's own ramp when a logged history exists and the generic `buildWarmupRamp` otherwise. End-to-end verified (`test/analytics-edge.test.js`: Bench with a logged ramp history → personalized 2-step 15/10 ramp; a lift with none → generic 8/5/3) + `buildPersonalizedRamp`/wiring unit suites (`test/session-builder.test.js`). **Feature complete** (no-data → generic; logged ramp history → the lifter's logic). No write-path / schema / proof-field / parser / LLM change; warm-ups stay display-only. Opus 4.8.
  - **Deferred — factor shape *consistency* into confidence + use a representative/median shape** `[polish]` (PR #461 review): `confidence` reflects the number of ramped sessions and the learned `steps` come from the most-recent session only; a median/modal shape across recent ramped sessions plus a consistency term would be more robust for a lifter whose ramp varies. Today the builder uses any non-null shape (≥2 ramped sessions) — fine for a stable ramper.
- **Deferred — [#402](https://github.com/tc5v5s64ym-sketch/atlas-workout-updater/issues/402) Part 2: save-ready copy shown before sets are logged** `[correctness]` — Suggested workout preview showed "Say 'log it' to save your session" before any actual sets were logged. `buildSuggestedWorkout` is not yet wired into a route, so the surface this appeared on is unclear. Revisit and get a precise repro when the `/api/suggest-workout` route lands. **Suggested PR type:** frontend display condition; Sonnet 4.6 if the fix is purely a visibility gate on `sessionLog.length > 0`.
Active roadmap cross-reference: `docs/ACTIVE_ROADMAP.md` currently sequences the trust-first queue as Steps 379-385. Keep this list and the roadmap in sync; `BACKLOG.md` remains the source of truth.

- **Deferred (discovered in PR #412 review): Orphaned deload state if session is abandoned mid-way** `[reliability]` — `advance` only fires when the user explicitly ends the planned session (`endPlannedSession`) AND `deloadWritten` is true. If the user logs sets during a deload then navigates away / refreshes without ending the session, `Deload_State` stays in `DELOAD_ACTIVE` indefinitely. The next `begin` will 409 (absorbed silently), so deloads can't restart until state is manually resolved. Acceptable for a single-owner app in the near term; future fix could auto-advance on app init if the persisted state is DELOAD_ACTIVE and the last training date is recent. **Suggested PR type:** small `public/app.js` + `/api/deload/status` check on load; low priority.
- **Deferred (discovered in PR #412 review): `/api/deload/begin` hardcodes `focus: 'strength'`** `[housekeeping]` — so only `STRENGTH_DELOAD_V1` (0.92 load) is ever invoked from the frontend. `selectProtocol` already supports hypertrophy/power/endurance, but the intent data doesn't currently expose a per-lift training focus. The strength default is the most conservative choice and safe for now. Future work: derive `focus` from the deload_reset intent or a user preference so the other protocols become reachable. **Suggested PR type:** small `public/app.js` + intent data change; Sonnet 4.6.
- **Deferred (discovered in PR #411 review): `/api/coaching/insights` uses mismatched stall windows** `[housekeeping]` — the route computes `stalls` via `detectStalls(allLog, 3)` but `deload_suggestions` via `suggestDeloads(allLog, 4)` (index.js ~1931-1932), so the stalls list and the deload list can disagree on which lifts qualify (3- vs 4-session window). Pre-existing, benign; align the `minSessions` threshold (or document why they differ). **Suggested PR type:** one-line `index.js` threshold alignment + test; Sonnet 4.6. (index.js is restricted — name it in scope first.)
- **Deferred (SESSION_DESIGN AC5a): liftCode history-merge for pre-override rows** `[correctness]` — Old rows written before `knownLiftCodeOverrides` (e.g. "LRX01" generated from initials before "LRA01" was added as canonical) will still resolve under the old code and miss the merged history. Fix: at read time in `normalizeLogRow`, look up the exercise name in `canonicalLiftCodeFor` and overwrite the stored liftCode when a canonical mapping exists. Blocked on confirming no test relies on the old generated code passing through unchanged (tracked: exerciseBenchmark tests use "LPD01" for Lat Pulldown — need to confirm canonicalLiftCodeFor would return the same). **Suggested PR type:** isolated `services/exerciseEnrichment.js` change + test confirmation; Sonnet 4.6 if tests confirm safety. **Note (Roadmap Step 376):** this is the *merge gap* (same exercise, history split across codes → own history missing). The opposite direction — *contamination* (different exercises sharing one code → foreign history leaking into the coach) — is now handled in `enrichCoachFacts` via `cleanLogForLift` (`services/liveIntelligence.js`). The merge gap remains open: Step 376 scopes history to the target exercise but does not re-unify a split code.

## Then

- **Phase 2 hardening (from [`FIX_PLAN.md`](./FIX_PLAN.md)):** `[polish]` friendly errors + no contradictory panels (ME-1/2/3). **Suggested PR type:** frontend-only polish; Sonnet 4.6.
### AUDIT.md reconciliation — still-live findings (verified against main 2026-06-20)

The 2026-06-14 code audit ([`AUDIT.md`](./AUDIT.md)) was never folded into this backlog the way `FIX_PLAN.md` was. Most of its Critical/High items have since been fixed organically (CR-1, CR-2, HI-1, HI-2, HI-3, HI-5, HI-6, HI-7, HI-8, ME-7, ME-8 — all verified FIXED). The items below were re-verified as **still live** against current `main` and are now tracked here:

- ✅ **ME-13 — two safety rules tested but never wired** `[correctness]` (2026-06-24, owner-approved "wire both") — `rirDrift` (RIR declining at the same load across sessions) and `checkE1rmJump` (>15% e1RM jump vs the lift's last-session best → typo guard) were fully built + unit-tested but dark: `evaluateSessionSafety` only ran `rirCaution`/`junkRepGuard`/`painFlag`, and `checkE1rmJump` had no `index.js` caller — green tests gave false confidence the warnings shipped. **Fix:** `evaluateSessionSafety(newRows, notes, historyRows)` gained an **optional** third arg; when history is supplied it runs `rirDrift` + the e1RM typo guard per distinct lift in `newRows` and emits them as `decision()` `rule_flags` (`rir_drift`, `e1rm_jump`) — non-blocking, never rejects a log; omitting history preserves the original two-arg behavior so existing callers are unaffected. Wired into the **`/api/log-workout` dry-run preview (`test_mode`) branch only** (`index.js`): it reads `Log_Cleaned` once, normalizes via `normalizeAnalyticsLogRow`, and augments `ruleFlags` before the preview body; best-effort (a history-read failure keeps the row-local flags). The **live-write path is intentionally untouched** — no extra Sheets read on the write hot path, no proof-field / `test_mode` / trust-loop / schema change. The previewed session id is excluded from the jump baseline so a set never compares against itself. Tests: 4 unit (`test/rules.test.js`) + 1 integration (`test/api-smoke.test.js`: 285×5 Bench vs 225×5 history → `e1rm_jump` flag, no append). Full suite green (2605).
  - **Deferred (ME-13 follow-up)** `[correctness]`: the `/api/complete-workout` path (`index.js:~2937`, Apple Watch screenshot completion) also builds `rule_flags` via `evaluateSessionSafety` but still passes only two args, so it does not get the history-aware guards. Scoped out to keep ME-13 one-concern (the backlog item named "the log-workout preview"). Wire history there too if the completion surface should also flag fatigue drift / typos — it would need the same `getSheetRows(logSheetName)` read threaded in. Low priority; Opus 4.8 (touches `index.js`).

The **still-live** findings are folded into the sub-items below (verified again against current `main` when filed). LO-1 is by-design-accepted (undo `dataIndex = r - 2` arithmetic verified correct for the current sheet shape in `docs/AUDIT_2026-06-12.md` — a *critical behaviour*, owner-gated, no new entry); LO-2 was **not exhaustively re-verified**; LO-3/LO-4/LO-10 are folded into the existing **"Phase 3 dead-code cleanup"** item (not duplicated). (ME-7 fixed via Step 384; ME-1/2/3 under "Phase 2 hardening" above.)
  - ✅ **ME-5 — unbounded `/api/complete-workout` body** `[correctness/security]` (2026-06-24) — two guards added: (1) multer `limits.fieldSize = 512 KB` so the `log_rows_json` *field* (not a file, so `fileSize` never bounded it) can't reach `JSON.parse` as a multi-megabyte string; (2) after the array check, reject `parsedLogRows.length > 200` with a 400 (`standardError`, temp file cleaned up) so an oversized array can't drive unbounded row-by-row enrichment (DoS). 200 is a generous ceiling (a real session is tens of rows); 512 KB is far above a real payload. Write-path-adjacent route, but **guard-only** — no change to the write logic, `test_mode`/proof fields, enrichment, or trust loop. The error middleware also now maps multer's `LIMIT_FIELD_VALUE` (an over-`fieldSize` field) to a clean **413**, matching the existing `LIMIT_FILE_SIZE` path instead of falling through to a generic 500 (PR #553 review). api-smoke tests: a 201-row payload → 400 (`200-row limit`); a >512 KB field → 413; both with **no append**. Full suite green (2600).
  - ✅ **ME-6 — rate-limiter never prunes; key proxy-spoofable** `[housekeeping/security]` (2026-06-24) — `rateLimitMiddleware` (`middleware.js`) now prunes expired windows (`resetAt <= currentTime`) from the `hits` Map at the start of each non-OPTIONS request, so the Map can't grow unbounded (one entry per distinct `name:ip` previously lived forever). Behaviour-preserving: an expired entry is already treated as a fresh window on next access, so pruning frees memory without changing any rate-limit decision. The proxy-hop concern is already satisfied — `app.set('trust proxy', 1)` (`index.js:148`) makes `req.ip` the real one-hop client IP. Added a read-only `trackedKeyCount()` introspection hook for the test. Test in `test/security.test.js` (3 distinct clients tracked → 1 after expiry + a new request; returning client still gets a fresh window). Full suite green (2597). No write-path / schema / proof-field / trust-loop change.
    - **Deferred (review note, PR #551)** `[housekeeping]`: the prune scans the whole `hits` Map on every request (O(n) per call). Harmless for the single-owner deployment where the Map stays tiny, but if request volume / distinct-key cardinality ever grows, switch to an amortized strategy (lazy prune gated on a counter/time threshold, or a bounded LRU eviction) to avoid the per-request scan. No action unless it matters.
  - ✅ **ME-9 — `propose_edit` numbers validated server-side (defense-in-depth)** `[trust-critical]` (2026-06-24) — `isValidEditSchema` (`services/coach.js`) now validates any **present** `weight`/`reps`/`rir` in an `add_set`/`update_set` proposal via new `editNumbersValid`: weight (lb) finite & `0 < w ≤ 2000`, reps integer `1..100`, rir finite `0..10` — rejecting NaN/Infinity/negative/zero/string/out-of-range. Each field is checked **only when present** (a bare `add_set` and a partial `update_set` stay valid — the client still bounds index vs visible rows and the write path re-parses; this is an added guard, not a replacement). A reply proposing an absurd number is stripped to prose with `propose_edit:null` and never reaches the approval gate. Reclassified autonomous (Escalation Policy v2: strengthens the trust contract, doesn't amend an INVARIANT). Tests in `test/coach.test.js` (valid numbers pass on each action; garbage weight/reps/rir rejected; back-compat for absent fields; end-to-end parser drop). Full suite green. No write-path / schema / proof-field / trust-loop change.

Documented default unchanged, so behavior is identical unless the env var is set. Reclassified autonomous (Escalation Policy v2: makes an already-documented env mechanism work — correctness, not a model-selection change; the default model is untouched). Tests in `test/vision.test.js` (default + override). Suite green.
  - ✅ **ME-11 — vision JSON mode + fence strip + abort timeout (both providers)** `[correctness]` (2026-06-24) — OpenAI Responses call adds `text:{format:{type:'json_object'}}` and now strips code fences defensively before parse; Gemini call adds `config:{responseMimeType:'application/json'}`. Both run through new `withVisionTimeout(fn, ms)` which threads a real `AbortSignal` into the SDK (`{signal}` for OpenAI, `config.abortSignal` for Gemini), surfaces a clear "timed out" error, and always clears the timer. Timeout is vision-appropriate (default 30s, `ATLAS_VISION_TIMEOUT_MS` override) rather than the coach's 8s — image parsing is heavier, and an over-aggressive cap would false-fail normal screenshots (the goal is bounding a hung call, not racing a fast one). Reclassified autonomous (defensive parsing/reliability, not product scope). Tests: fence strip, timeout abort + clear error, non-timeout error passthrough, signal threading. Suite green.
  - ✅ **ME-12 — `dotenv` hard `require` at load** `[housekeeping]` (2026-06-24) — `index.js:1-2` no longer hard-requires `dotenv` (`const dotenv = require('dotenv'); dotenv.config();`), which would crash module load if `dotenv` were absent. Now `try { require('dotenv').config(); } catch { /* env injected by the host */ }` — dotenv is a local-dev convenience only; Render injects env vars directly. Source-introspection guard test in `test/unit.test.js` (no hard top-level require; `dotenv.config()` wrapped in try/catch). Full suite green (2598). No write-path / schema / proof-field / trust-loop change.

Converted all three to `standardError(req, res, '<public msg>', NODE_ENV === 'production' ? null : err.message, 400)` — production now returns only the public message (no internal detail), dev keeps the detail, and the shape matches the route's other error responses (the frontend `api()` helper already reads `json.message || json.error`, so no client change). Source-introspection guard test in `test/unit.test.js` pins the three sites. No write-logic / proof-field / `test_mode` / schema / trust-loop change — only error verbosity. Full suite green (2596). **Remaining LO-2 scope** (broad envelope-consistency audit across all routes) stays deferred; this closes the concrete leak.

What fails: pending exercises queued before a restart are silently lost. Why it matters: user sees no preview on next request. **DECISION (owner 2026-06-25): DOCUMENT THE LIMITATION — accepted as a known limitation at solo/single-user scale. No Sheet write-surface added unless the limitation demonstrably bites in live use.** See [FIX_PLAN.md "Additional verified items"](./FIX_PLAN.md).

- **Session-state trust repair (Roadmap Steps 372–377) now active — see `docs/ACTIVE_ROADMAP.md`.** Sequenced from the June 2026 app-test failures: substitution state engine (372 ✅) → coach reads authoritative live session (373 ✅) → apply accepted swap into live session (373b ✅) → planned names always loggable (374 ✅) → coach "what's left" reads authoritative state (375 ✅) → suppress cross-lift history (376 ✅) → deterministic session-close fallback (377 ✅). The 372–377 series is now complete. Steps 355–371 (prior session-execution + intelligence + voice series) are ✅ complete — see the completed table in the roadmap.
  - **Deferred (Step 377)** `[correctness, edge-case]` — the session-close deterministic fallback lives server-side in `/api/coach/chat` (both the unconfigured early-return and the error/timeout catch answer close questions from `computePlanState`). The one path it does NOT cover is a pure *client-side* timeout: `getChatReply` in `public/coach-conversation.js` races the request against `COACH_LLM_TIMEOUT_MS` (9s) and resolves `{message:null}` if the server is slower, after which `chatFallback` shows the generic "Coach is unavailable." In practice the server's Gemini timeout (8s) fires first, so the server returns its engine answer before the client gives up. A client-side mirror would require replicating `computePlanState` in the browser IIFE (it can't `require()` the service) — the same keep-in-sync anti-pattern flagged for `applySessionSubstitution`/`nextExerciseFromPlan` — so it was intentionally left out. **Suggested PR:** if the edge ever bites, either raise the client timeout above the server's, or add a `chatFallback` session-close branch fed by a UMD-wrapped shared `computePlanState`. Low priority; Sonnet 4.6.
  - **Deferred (Step 376)** `[correctness, edge-case]` — `cleanLogForLift` variant matching falls back to `canonicalLiftCodeFor`, which only resolves names in the small `knownLiftCodeOverrides` table (does NOT include Leg Extension, Leg Press, Hack Squat, etc.). If the same exercise is logged under two spellings whose `canonical_exercise` differs (e.g. "Leg Extension" vs "Leg Extensions") AND a foreign lift contaminates the code, the off-spelling rows match neither the normalized name nor a canonical code and get dropped — losing legitimate history. In practice unlikely: `rowName()` prefers col 3 (`canonical_exercise`), which enrichment normally writes consistently, so `distinctCanon.size` stays 1 and the function no-ops; and the drop is the safe direction (suppress rather than cite foreign numbers). **Suggested PR:** broaden variant matching beyond the override table (e.g. plural/stemming normalization), or fix it at the root via the AC5a read-time liftCode unification above. Low priority; Sonnet 4.6.

Three of these (log-vs-ask intent, mid-session stats, conversational undo) are also flagged under **Atlas voice & interaction** above as voice-priority work. ## Operational resilience — NEEDS TRIAGE (not yet scoped)

A category the backlog has not modelled. Atlas's source of truth is Google Sheets with **no database**, so the runtime plumbing around reads/writes carries real data-trust risk that the coaching-intelligence work does not cover. None of these are on fire today (single user, low concurrency), but they are unguarded.

- **Backup is manual-only** `[housekeeping]` (`scripts/export-sheets-backup.js`): a read-only export script exists but nothing schedules it. **DECISION (owner 2026-06-25): ACCEPTED — manual-only backup is the correct posture at solo/single-user scale. Revisit before broader beta.** See `docs/BACKUP_ROLLBACK.md`.
- **Render cold start kills the first request after idle — diagnosed live 2026-07-02** `[correctness]` — Owner live re-test (IMG_5450, 9:27): the first screenshot attach of the session died with **"Preview failed: Load failed"** (iOS Safari's transport-level fetch failure). Diagnosed with an authed dry-run against production: the first `/api/complete-workout` request took **36s wall-clock with only ~3s of server processing** (cold instance spin-up + a slow first vision call); the next three requests took 1.4–5.9s. Mobile Safari gives up on a hanging upload → "Load failed." The trust loop held (nothing saved, honest failure) but the copy is unhelpful and the pattern WILL recur — the owner's usage (idle between gym sessions) means the first interaction of most sessions risks a cold instance. Mitigations (a)+(b) ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-02 (composer-first Phase 0b): dry-run-only retry-once in `api()` + the honest waking-up copy at both Preview-failed surfaces; (c) remains an owner infra decision. Original options: (a) **client retry-once on transport failure for DRY-RUN previews only** — safe (test_mode is idempotent, no write; the live write must NEVER auto-retry) and closes most of the visible failure; (b) friendlier network-failure copy ("The server was waking up — try again" instead of "Load failed"); (c) keep-alive ping or a paid Render tier (owner infra decision). Related-but-separate: the LLM provider-resilience item below (#586) covers provider failures; this is instance cold start.
- **Provider resilience and cost-aware LLM routing (GitHub issue [#586](https://github.com/tc5v5s64ym-sketch/atlas-workout-updater/issues/586))** `[trust-critical]` — **Trigger: after P0 workout reliability / Active Workout State Unification is stable in live use. Do not promote early.** A single provider quota event (Gemini free-tier 429, June 2026) simultaneously broke coaching voice AND screenshot saves — two architecture defects exposed: (1) the coach voice was load-bearing (provider failure reached the user flow); (2) no error boundary (raw provider JSON reached the client). Full design rationale, June 2026 provider scoring, error boundary spec, and implementation PR sequence: [`docs/LLM_ARCHITECTURE.md`](./docs/LLM_ARCHITECTURE.md). Eight-PR sequence in boundary-first order: (1) error boundary + friendly degradation — closes the incident root cause without any provider switching; (2) provider interface + OpenAI adapter — behavior-preserving refactor; (3) fallback provider + Groq adapter — `gpt-4.1-nano` primary / `llama-3.1-8b-instant` fallback (cross-vendor resilience, both OpenAI-compatible); (4) cost-aware tier router — Tier 1 cheap/fast vs Tier 2 capable, deterministic selection; (5) determinism guard — test fails if LLM call is reachable from parse/log/save/mutation; (6) circuit breaker + per-provider cooldown; (7) structured-output hardening + server-side validation; (8) observability. Monthly cost at current volume after all PRs: ~$1.

---

## Test-coverage hardening — make Atlas safer without building new behaviour (owner-approved 2026-06-29)

Initiative scope: **test-infrastructure only.** No runtime behaviour changes, no "fixing while testing." If a test exposes broken current behaviour, **stop and report** — it becomes a separate bug-fix PR with owner/ledger handling, never patched inside a coverage PR. Source of findings: 2026-06-29 coverage analysis (`node --test --experimental-test-coverage`, 3,015 tests, ~96.8% overall line; gaps concentrated in `sheets.js` 31%/15% func, `index.js` 62% branch, `services/vision.js` 57%, untested frontend `chat.js`/`drawer.js`/`nav.js`/`sw.js`). Owner sequencing decision recorded below.

- ✅ **PR1 — `sheets.js` adapter contract tests** `[housekeeping]` (this PR) — fake-`googleapis` injection runs the REAL adapter in-memory (no live sheet). Covers: `deleteRowsByRange` row-index math + sheetId-by-title resolution (the undo backbone), `appendRows` range/`RAW`/`INSERT_ROWS` shape + empty-payload guard, `readRange`/`getSheetRows` range construction + header-strip + maxRows slicing, `getPrivateKey` `\n` un-escaping, `ensureSheetTab` (creates missing tab by correct title, seeds header only when empty, never clobbers an existing header). `test/sheets-adapter.test.js`, 11 tests. Lifted `sheets.js` from 31%→71% line, 15%→58% func. No source change.
  - ✅ **PR1b — `sheets.js` read-helper tests** `[housekeeping]` (shipped) — `test/sheets-adapter-reads.test.js` covers the remaining read helpers: `getRecentRows`, `getHeaderRow`, `getSpreadsheetTabs`, `getExerciseCatalog`, `getEffortSessionIds`, `getLogCompositeKeys` (composite-key build + header-skip + lowercasing). `getColumnValues` is module-private (not exported) so its range/`COLUMNS`/first-column contract is covered transitively via `getEffortSessionIds`/`getLogCompositeKeys`. Same fake-`googleapis` harness as PR1. Combined `sheets.js` coverage now ~88% line / ~81% func (the rest — `validateConfig`/`isTransientAppendError`/`retryWithBackoff` — is covered in `test/unit.test.js`). No source change.
- **PR2 — `index.js` write-path negative/branch tests** `[trust-critical]` — owner decision: **SPLIT**. **Current-State Verification Gate finding (2026-06-29): PR2a/PR2b/PR2c were already covered in `test/api-smoke.test.js`** — building them would have been duplicate tests, so they are marked stale rather than implemented. Only PR2d was a genuine gap and shipped.
  - ◼︎ **PR2a — STALE / already covered** — `/api/log-workout` negative + proof-field cases already exist: `test_mode` dry-run proof (api-smoke "log-workout test_mode returns dry-run proof without append"), invalid-payload-no-append, implausible-RIR 400, live append + recomputed volume, header-reorder fail-closed (400/500), `write_id`-required 400, dry-run does-not-consume-idempotency. No new PR.
  - ◼︎ **PR2b — STALE / already covered** — `/api/log-workout/undo-last` cases already exist: wrong-tab 400, `rows_to_delete` mismatch 400, missing `log_appended_range` 400, session_id mismatch 409-no-delete, target-row-missing 409-no-delete. No new PR.
  - ◼︎ **PR2c — STALE / already covered** — `/api/complete-workout` cases already exist: oversized `log_rows_json` (>200) 400-no-append, duplicate session 409-no-Effort-write, dry-run no-409 graceful preview, partial effort-failure (log appended, effort fails, retry never re-appends), invalid-screenshot-metrics degrade. No new PR.
  - ✅ **PR2d — `/api/constraints` 503-until-tab-exists** `[trust-critical]` (shipped) — the one real gap: constraints had GET/POST/validation/idempotency/manifest tests but no test for the documented 503-until-Constraints-tab-exists path. Added to `test/api-smoke.test.js` (+ a `hideConstraintsTab` flag in the fake `getSpreadsheetTabs`, mirroring `hideModalityLogTab`/`hideDeloadStateTab`): asserts 503 + "Constraints tab not found" message + nothing written, AND that the 503 path releases the `write_id` (`failWrite`) so a retry after the tab exists is not a duplicate and writes exactly once. Revisit only if pure helpers are later factored out.
  - ◼︎ **PR4c — DEFERRED (low unit-test value)** — `public/drawer.js` (195 lines, ~39 DOM ops) and `public/nav.js` (578 lines, ~113 DOM ops) are DOM + `api()` fetch glue with no extractable pure logic; e2e is the right vehicle. Revisit if helpers are factored out.
  - ◼︎ **PR4d — DEFERRED (owner, lowest priority)** — service-worker `public/sw.js` cache/offline tests. Owner deferred; the trust loop matters more than the PWA cache right now.
- ✅ **PR5 — CI coverage report (non-blocking)** `[housekeeping]` (shipped) — added a `coverage-report` job to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` that runs `node --test --experimental-test-coverage` and writes the summary (overall + key source files: index.js / sheets.js / vision.js / coach.js / analytics.js / workoutTextParser.js) to the GitHub Actions run summary. **Report-only, `continue-on-error: true` — never gates a merge** (owner decision: a global threshold now would be a false sense of safety since the high overall % masks low-coverage critical files, and a strict per-file threshold would fail immediately). A hard gate is intentionally deferred until the coverage-hardening PRs settle — owner's later target: global lines 95% / branches 80% / functions 85%, no per-file threshold yet, exclude generated/coverage output (and `scripts/` only if separately justified).

New `test/coach-gemini.test.js` stubs the global `fetch` (no network) to drive: success (text returned), non-OK status, empty-output → the three `callGeminiContents` outcomes; the unconfigured guard (no `GEMINI_API_KEY`) throwing before any fetch; `generateCoachMessage`/`generatePlanMessage`/`pingGemini`/`generateChatReply` success; and `generateVerdictReaction`'s `shouldReactToVerdict` gate (null + no call on a quiet "met" set vs a "beat" verdict that calls). 10 tests. Read-only voice; no write path; `coach.js` unchanged.

## Housekeeping

- **Curl taxonomy + multi-curl clarify (follow-ups to the bare-lead disambiguation PR)** `[correctness]` (owner-reserved): (1) the catalog has near-duplicate bicep-curl rows — "Bicep Curl" (BC01) and "Dumbbell Curl" (CRL01) — for effectively the same movement. This PR steers plain "curl"/"curls" to BC01 and the plan-aware client layer resolves bare terms to the planned curl, but "dumbbell curl" still resolves to its own CRL01 row. Folding Dumbbell Curl into Bicep Curl is a product/logging-model decision (recommendation engine names "Dumbbell Curl") — owner call; either merge the catalog rows or add `dumbbell curl → bicep curl` to `PREFERRED_ALIAS_TARGETS`. (2) When the plan has MULTIPLE exercises of one family (e.g. bicep + hammer curls), a bare "curls" is left unresolved (falls to the alias default). Owner floated an optional "ask which one" clarify step — build the clarify UX if the multi-curl case proves common. Both in `services/exerciseEnrichment.js` / `public/app.js`.
- **Completed-exercise read-back — lift-code / alias matching (follow-up to the plural-name fix)** `[correctness]`: `computePlanState` now matches completion by singular-normalized name + lift_code, which fixes the plural class ("Lateral Raises" ↔ "Lateral Raise"). It does NOT resolve abbreviations/aliases ("OHP" → "Overhead Press") or a substituted lift completing its plan slot ("Lateral Raise" logged against a "Face Pull" slot) — those surfaced in the live read-back bug (FR-20260706013655-4t692wuh, "It read back to me a couple workouts I already did… when we were talking about laterals"). The safe fix is to thread the authoritative **lift_code** end-to-end: `currentPlanForChat` and `plan_completed` currently send NAMES only (`public/app.js`), so the lift_code tier in `computePlanState` is never exercised for the chat's `plan_state`. Have the client send `{name, liftCode}` for `current_plan` and `plan_completed` (codes are available via the recommendation / `resolveCompletedIdentity`) so completion matches by code regardless of name form — no fuzzy guessing (which risks marking the WRONG exercise done). Touches the restricted `public/app.js` session surface → own focused PR; owner may want to pick the approach (lift-code vs. adding a conservative fuzzy tier).
- **Confirm-card "already saved this session" recap — two follow-ups (from PR #863 review)** `[housekeeping]`: (1) the `sessionSavedLog` ledger is in-memory only and not part of the session snapshot, so a page reload / PWA eviction after a mid-session save resets the recap to empty — the earlier-saved exercises would again look absent from the closeout card (data is safe on the Sheet; display-only convenience doesn't survive a reload). Persist it into the session snapshot if the reload case matters. (2) The recap sources exercise names from `sessionLog` at approve time rather than from the written payload (`collectLogRows`/edit table), so if the owner edits/removes rows in the preview before approving, the recap could list a slightly different name than what was written. Both display-only, low-impact; capture only. Suggested PR type: small `public/app.js` follow-up, own concern.
- **Flight Recorder `latestCoachMessage()` picks the last `.coach-msg` across all bubbles** `[housekeeping]` (from PR #864 review): a single coach bubble can hold multiple `.coach-msg` nodes (ack / effort-note / extra), so the captured `coach_message` may be a trailing effort-note rather than the main reply. Still a truthful "most-recent visible coach text" (acceptable for observability); if tighter fidelity is wanted, pick the FIRST `.coach-msg` of the LAST `.chat-bubble-atlas`. Display-only, non-blocking.
- **Close obsolete PR [#288](https://github.com/tc5v5s64ym-sketch/atlas-workout-updater/pull/288)** (superseded by [#290](https://github.com/tc5v5s64ym-sketch/atlas-workout-updater/pull/290)). ✅ Already closed 2026-06-16.
- **Branch protection on `main`** `[housekeeping]` (GitHub Settings → Branches): require PRs + required status checks + "require branches to be up to date before merging." What fails without it: stale branches can silently clobber shared files on merge. Why it matters: prevents accidental data loss in the shared handoff bus. Suggested PR type: GitHub settings change (no code); owner action only.
- **Coach-voice golden-output rubric** `[housekeeping]`: Extend `test/coach.test.js` into a rubric where a voice change must update the test — ends the per-PR phrasing loop. See [FIX_PLAN.md "Process changes"](./FIX_PLAN.md). What fails without it: voice changes pass tests without proving the new wording is correct. Suggested PR type: test-only PR; Sonnet 4.6.
- ◐ **Phase 3 dead-code cleanup** `[housekeeping]` (optional, low-priority): ✅ redundant condition `if (!previousPerformance.completed && previousPerformance.completed === false)` (`services/recommendationPolicy.js:143`) — AUDIT LO-10 — **done** (simplified to `completed === false`, behavior-identical for every value; regression tests added for the missed-target hold + the completed-set guard). ✅ **stale unused imports in `index.js` analytics destructure** (`buildWeeklyReport`/`buildProgressSummary`/`buildExerciseDetail`/`buildMuscleGroupReadiness` — each imported but never referenced; verified count=1) — AUDIT LO-4 — **done (this PR).** **Remaining (LO-3, deferred — NOT cleanly dead):** `loadTodaysPlan()` (`public/app.js`, targets the removed `#todays-plan` per `docs/TODAY_SCREEN_SPEC.md`) is uncalled at runtime, **but 7 source-introspection tests in `test/unit.test.js` pin its plan-card rendering** ("today's plan…", "start-any-lift: plan cards are tappable and call startLift", "Tap to start hint", etc.). Removing it cleanly therefore also means removing those 7 tests AND confirming the plan-card / `startLift` UI is truly gone for good (it touches the removed Today/Dashboard screen — Dashboard restoration is on the do-NOT-build list, so likely safe, but it's a deliberate call, not silent housekeeping). Do it as its own focused PR with that owner-aware framing; left in place here rather than hastily deleting tested behavior. - **Dead enrichment alias `Lats → Lat Pulldown` in `exerciseEnrichment.js`** `[housekeeping]`: PR #335 demoted "lats" to a contextual alias — the parser now surfaces `needs_clarification` instead of emitting a "Lats" unknown-exercise row, so the enrichment-layer mapping is effectively unreachable for parser-originated rows. Harmless for now; remove in a future cleanup PR. Suggested PR type: one-line delete + regression test; Sonnet 4.6.
- **API key in `localStorage`** `[housekeeping]` (`public/app.js:9`): XSS exposure risk; safe for single-user Dale, becomes a real item at multi-user/productization. Logged here so it's not a surprise. See [FIX_PLAN.md "Additional verified items"](./FIX_PLAN.md). Suggested PR type: future security hardening (not urgent for single-user tool; revisit at productization).
- **JSON `/api/log-workout` write path has no `log_rows.length` upper bound** `[housekeeping]` (`index.js` ~4022): the `MAX_LOG_ROWS` (200) cap lives only in the multipart `/api/complete-workout` handler (`parsedLogRows`). So the shared write/verify/undo ceiling (raised in the verify/undo row-span fix) is not *literally* end-to-end — a single JSON `/api/log-workout` write of >200 rows would append but then 400 on `verify-range` / `undo-last`. Realistically unreachable for one session (surfaced as a non-blocking review note on the verify/undo-span PR), but for the "all three bounded by one constant" invariant to hold end-to-end, apply the same `MAX_LOG_ROWS` guard to `log_rows.length` in the JSON handler. Suggested PR type: tiny write-path guard + regression test; own concern (a write-path behavior change, deliberately kept out of the verify/undo-span PR).

Catches the raw token under any var name, same approach as the existing `google-ai-api-key` rule. 5 tests added to `test/secret-scan.test.js` (rule exists, flags a committed/leaked token, does not flag the setup-instructions ellipsis in `claude-code-review.yml` or a too-short lookalike); all token values built at runtime so the test file itself never contains a literal matchable token. Scripts-only; no app/schema/write-path change.

## Multi-style training support — PLANNING (owner-gated, not active)

Atlas is currently shaped around one lifter's style (gym progressive overload in `weight × reps @ RIR`). The Dream is a training-intelligence engine for **many** styles. Planning spec filed: [`docs/TRAINING_PROFILE_TAXONOMY.md`](./docs/TRAINING_PROFILE_TAXONOMY.md) — defines the first five training profiles (strength · hypertrophy · general fitness · cardio/endurance · bodyweight/calisthenics/circuits), a deterministic **profile-score** model (style as a multi-axis vector, not one fixed label), an onboarding classifier, an exercise **modality schema** (`load_type`/`fatigue_class`/`effort_metric`/`progression_metric`), expanded logging grammar (duration/distance/pace/zone/rounds/`+load`), a **profile-aware stimulus governor** (RIR rules differ by profile; RIR is irrelevant for cardio), live cross-modality fatigue routing, coach-voice examples, and a per-profile golden test matrix. Research-grounded (WHO 2020 / HHS activity guidelines; ACSM progression; Zourdos 2016 RIR-RPE; Refalo 2023 proximity-to-failure; HR-zone / Karvonen models).

**Status:** docs/plan only — no production, write-path, parser, or schema change. **Owner-gated to promote.** Phased engine-first build plan (PR-T2…PR-T8) lives in the spec's Part D. Scope fence: this is multi-*style* for the single owner, **not** multi-*user* (multi-user stays out per the Vision guardrail). Promote a slice into `docs/ACTIVE_ROADMAP.md` only on explicit owner direction.

- **PR-T2** modality schema (pure data) `[correctness]` · **PR-T3** profile-score engine (read-only) `[correctness]` · **PR-T4** onboarding classifier `[correctness]` · **PR-T5** profile-aware stimulus governor `[trust-critical]` · **PR-T6** expanded logging grammar — **owner-gated** (slash-notation contract / `workoutTextParser.js`) + the **[owner-gated migration]** decision for where non-RIR metrics persist `[trust-critical]` · **PR-T7** live fatigue routing `[correctness]` · **PR-T8** coach voice `[polish]`.

**Session Planning Engine — PLANNING (owner-gated, not active).** Planning spec filed: [`docs/SESSION_PLANNING_ENGINE.md`](./docs/SESSION_PLANNING_ENGINE.md) — documents how Atlas picks the **best session today** (not just "what's next"): daily **objective-scoring model** (`profile_match`/`goal_match`/`equipment_fit`/`time_fit`/`recovery_fit`/`adherence_fit`/`movement_balance_need` minus `pain_conflict`/`fatigue_conflict`; weights are initial defaults, fixture-protected before shipping), ten supported **session objectives** (strength_progression · hypertrophy_volume · general_fitness_balanced · cardio_base · cardio_intervals · bodyweight_strength · circuit_conditioning · skill_practice · recovery_reload · deload), deterministic **exercise-order rules** + exceptions, planning + fatigue-adjusted examples, a golden-fixture test matrix, and the boundaries vs the Taxonomy / Modality Schema / Stimulus Governor / Live Fatigue Router / Coach Voice / Recovery-Deload systems. Builds on the Training Profile Taxonomy (the planner is the profile-aware front end over the existing `scoreIntents` / `structureSession` machinery). Also documents a **recovery/deload model** (grounded in the *Deloading in Resistance Training* report): convergence-based deload triggers (no deload after one bad session), default deload prescription, profile-aware deload styles, and the boundary between normal fatigue routing · `recovery_reload` · `deload` · taper · maintenance · complete rest — **planning-layer selection only; does not change `docs/DELOAD_SPEC.md` / `computePrescription`**. **Docs/plan only** — no production, parser, write-path, or schema change; **owner-gated to promote** (do not edit `docs/ACTIVE_ROADMAP.md` until promoted). Suggested first slices once promoted: objective-scoring engine (pure, read-only) → fixture-pin the weights → objective→order builder, deterministic-first.

### Training Intelligence Implementation Series (owner-approved slices)

The build sequence that implements the Training Profile Taxonomy + Session Planning Engine planning specs. **PR 477 is promoted to `docs/ACTIVE_ROADMAP.md` and is the only active slice.** All later slices stay **owner-gated / not active** — promote one at a time on explicit owner direction. (The `PR 47x` labels are the owner's planned *logical* slice numbers; actual GitHub PR numbers are a separate namespace and may differ — see the roadmap's "Roadmap Step numbers vs GitHub PR numbers" note.)

- **PR 477 — RIR-aware coach accountability + live pressing fatigue routing** `[trust-critical]` — **ACTIVE** (promoted). Behavior-changing, trust-sensitive; **current weighted/RIR workflow only**. Detects warmup/feeder vs work vs redline (RIR 0) sets, rep-drop-after-redline, high-RIR underdosed work sets; marks pressing (chest/triceps/front-delts · horizontal_push/vertical_push) intra-session yellow; routes the next planned press (pull-first reroute / cap / optional) after a bench redline. Reuses `effortVerdict`/`recommendNextSet`/`patternFor`/`musclesFor`/`classifyLiftRole` + the existing coach reaction channel. No parser/schema/write-path change.
  - ✅ **PR-A (engine, GitHub #478)** — pure `services/setEffortSignals.js` (`analyzeSetSequence`/`assessNextMoveConflict`) + fixtures, unwired. *Merged.*
  - ✅ **PR-B (wiring + deterministic copy)** — *shipped.* Reason codes registered centrally (`services/trainingKnowledge.js`); new pure `services/setEffortCopy.js` (deterministic `effortNote`/`rerouteNote`); engine run in the `/api/coach/message` set path (gated on weighted/RIR sets + a non-empty planned queue), returning additive `effort_note` + suggestion-only `reroute`; `public/app.js` threads the remaining `plannedQueue`; `public/coach-conversation.js` renders one short effort line + folds the reroute into the existing handoff. No parser/schema/write-path/trust-loop change; LLM prompt untouched. Tests: `test/setEffortCopy.test.js`, api-smoke live-path + `unit.test.js` source-introspection.
    - **Deferred (PR-B discoveries):**

      - `[correctness]` Cardio / bodyweight-only sets (no finite weight/RIR) produce **no** effort line by design — the set-effort engine is RIR/weight-only. Multi-modality effort reads land in **PR 486+**.

Align the placeholder with the reroute if live use wants it.

In `handleLogIt`, an EMPTY `sessionLog` AND a prior save this session (`lastWrite` set) now short-circuits with "Nothing new to log since your last save — log some sets first." (status `warn`), BEFORE the `/api/session/compile` fallback. This stops the post-save "log it" from (1) emitting an LLM "session logged / great work" closeout that implied a fresh write, and (2) recompiling the already-saved chat history — which risked re-writing sets already on the sheet. New sets logged after a save still buffer (`sessionLog` non-empty → the structured-buffer branch wins first), so a genuine second save is unaffected. Read-only guard; no write-path/schema/parser/modality change. SW shell v35→v36. Tests: `unit.test.js` source-introspection (nothing-new guard is `lastWrite`-gated, returns, precedes the compile fallback, and the `sessionLog` branch still precedes it). Full suite green (2539). **Live-audit plan (PR 1–3) complete.**
  - ✅ **Follow-up Fix A — multi-line, one-exercise-per-line strength logging** *(shipped, owner-authorized)*: a SECOND-round live test surfaced that a single multi-line message (`Bench 225 5/2 x3` / `Weighted dips +50 10/2 x3` / `Seated row 190 10/2 x3`) logged only ONE exercise. Root cause (confirmed): the backend INTENTIONALLY blocks a multi-exercise blob (`hasMultipleExerciseMentions` → `multiple_exercises_in_input`); since `extractSetParagraphs` splits only on BLANK lines, single-newline lines collapse to one multi-exercise line → blocked → the client (`noFallback`) dropped it to the coach, which summarized all three convincingly but buffered nothing (same masking class as Bugs #1/#2; new trigger). Fix (client-only, no backend-parser change): `rowsFromBackendParsedWorkout` flags `err.multipleExercises`; `rowsFromWorkoutInput` routes a `multiple_exercises_in_input` rejection to the existing line-based local parser **only when** the input spans newlines AND the local parse is CLEAN (no per-line errors) — so all lines log. **Same-line mixing (no newline) stays blocked** (backend guard untouched); an uncertain line is never silently logged (falls through). No write-path/schema/modality/coach-prompt change. SW shell v36→v37. Tests: behavioral (the pure local parser run on the exact failing input → 9 rows / 3 exercises / 0 errors; single line → 1 exercise) + source-introspection (branch gated on newline + clean parse, runs before the coach drop) + the existing backend same-line-block golden is preserved. Updated `clarification_blocks_local_parser_invocation` to permit ONLY this guarded exception. Full suite green (2542).
  - ✅ **Follow-up Fix B — bodyweight dips alias** *(shipped, owner-authorized)*: round-2 live test found `Dips 10/2 x3` returned "Could not find sets for Dips (Weighted)". Root cause: the `dips`/`dip` alias resolves to the canonical `Dips (Weighted)` (added load is the common case), forcing a weighted interpretation; with no weight token, `parseSetGroups` finds no sets → clarification. Fix (surgical, mirrors the existing `Hanging Knee Raises` bodyweight branch): in `parseLogSets`, when the resolved exercise is `Dips (Weighted)` AND the weighted slash parser finds **no** sets (no external load), re-label to a bodyweight `Dips` and read the reps via `parseBodyweightReps` (weight null). **Weighted dips unchanged** — `Dips +25 8/2` / `Weighted dips +50 10/2 x3` still resolve `Dips (Weighted)` with their load (parseSetGroups finds the load → the bodyweight branch is skipped). All Dips variants share lift code `DIP01` in `exerciseEnrichment.js`, so bodyweight vs added-load differ only by the weight value, not the exercise. No write-path/schema/modality/coach change; backend-only (no SW bump). Tests: `parser-golden.test.js` (bare `Dips`/`Dip` → bodyweight Dips weight-null; weighted dips preserved; mixed bodyweight + weighted + bench session). Full suite green (2546).

- **[Future enhancement, owner-gated] — Exercise-classification-aware fatigue model** `[training-intelligence, correctness]`

  **Status:** Deferred future enhancement. Not a regression or P0. Promote only on explicit owner direction.

  **Lane:** Training Intelligence (follows the PR 477–486 series). No active queue slot yet.

  **Live evidence (2026-06-26):** Heavy Back Squat sessions correctly leave Legs in a high-fatigue state. Accessory work such as Hanging Knee Raises currently contributes a similar "Worked" status for Core, even though the real systemic recovery cost is much lower. The recommendation and coaching surfaces treat both equally because the current readiness model sees only *when* a pattern was trained and *how hard* (via `minRir`), not *what kind of exercise* created that training stress.

  **Root cause (current design):**
  - `services/analytics.js::buildMuscleGroupReadiness` groups all logged sets by movement pattern and runs `recoveryFraction(daysSince, minRir, effortTauMultiplier(intensity))`. This correctly decays harder sessions slower (lower `minRir` → longer tau), but it does not distinguish a heavy compound at RIR 2 from an isolation accessory at the same RIR. A Knee Raise session with `minRir 2` and a Back Squat session with `minRir 2` produce the same recovery tau even though their systemic cost differs by an order of magnitude.
  - `services/muscleVolume.js::weeklyMuscleVolume` credits every set with a flat weight (1.0 primary / 0.5 secondary) regardless of exercise classification, load, or systemic cost.
  - `services/underCoverage.js::computeUnderCoverage` derives `adequate`/`under`/`optimal` status from total effective sets vs MEV targets — no exercise classification weighting. An accessory-only Core week can read as `adequate` even when no meaningful progressive overload was applied.

  **Existing infrastructure already in place (no new vocabulary needed):**
  - `services/exerciseModality.js::modalityFor` already emits a `fatigue_class` field (`high_cns`, `moderate_systemic`, `local`, `metabolic`, `aerobic`, `anaerobic`) for every exercise — but this field is currently unused in the readiness / volume / coverage / recommendation path.
  - `services/liftRole.js` classifies exercises as compound vs accessory; its module comment explicitly flags this as "a stopgap until the Exercise_Catalog carries explicit lift_role / fatigue_class metadata."
  - `services/stimulusGovernor.js::gradeStimulus` already applies different fatigue signals for heavy compounds (`high` on RIR 0) vs isolation (`elevated` or `ok`), and `services/stimulusGovernorRules.js` encodes those distinctions — the vocabulary and rules exist; they are just not wired into the readiness/recommendation path.
  - `recoveryFraction` in `services/analytics.js` already parameterizes the exponential decay tau by `minRir` and `effortTauMultiplier` — extending it with a third axis (`fatigue_class` multiplier) would be a small, contained change.

  **Desired future behavior:**
  - Heavy compounds (`high_cns`: Back Squat, Deadlift, Bench Press, Overhead Press) should produce a longer effective recovery tau — the readiness status reads `fatigued` or `recovering` for proportionally longer.
  - Accessories and isolation work (`local`: Knee Raises, Curls, Face Pulls, Shrugs) should produce a shorter tau — the same readiness status clears to `ready` / `fresh` in 1–2 days regardless of RIR.
  - `moderate_systemic` exercises (rows, dips, pull-ups, Romanian Deadlifts) fall between the two.
  - Workout recommendations (`scoreIntents`, `suggestedWorkout`) and coaching surfaces consume this richer fatigue model, so suggestions are based on meaningful recovery state rather than raw session recency.
  - No write-path, no schema, no parser change. Read-only engine enhancement only.

  **Proposed implementation direction (not designed or approved — speculative):**
  - Add a `fatigue_class` multiplier to `recoveryFraction`'s tau in `buildMuscleGroupReadiness`: derive `fatigue_class` via `modalityFor(canonical_exercise).fatigue_class` for the dominant (highest-volume) exercise in the session pattern bucket. Suggested initial ratios (fixture-protected before any wiring): `high_cns` → 1.4× tau, `moderate_systemic` → 1.0× (unchanged baseline), `local` → 0.7× tau, `metabolic`/`aerobic` → 0.5× tau.
  - Optionally introduce a parallel `weightedMuscleVolume` that applies the same `fatigue_class` multiplier to per-set effective-set credit in `muscleVolume.js`, consumed by `computeUnderCoverage` so the `adequate`/`under` status reflects exercise quality, not just set count.
  - The two changes are separable: the readiness tau extension is smaller-scoped and higher-signal; the volume credit extension is broader. Build the tau extension first.

  **Why deferred:** Not a regression — Atlas gives broadly correct recommendations today, and the existing `minRir`-based tau already captures the hardest effort in a session. This is a depth enhancement: the gap is between a hard compound session and a hard isolation session at the same RIR, which the current model cannot distinguish. Requires owner direction, a design pass on multiplier values, and golden-fixture protection before any wiring. Build after the active P0 queue and Trust-Critical Coach Interaction Lane are stable.

  **Owner gate:** Promote to the active roadmap and approve the `fatigue_class` tau multiplier table before implementation. This is a new training-intelligence scope addition, not a repair of a failing behavior.
